## The goal

One search box. The person types a question and presses Enter; the engine behind the box decides which source answers
(holiday data, employee directory, health check-up schedule, circulars, or an executive's written summary). The **Executive
Deep Synthesis (EDS) button is removed**, and the service behind it stays uninterrupted, accurate and well presented.

## The problem list (from the last chat: 20 designed queries + the 60+ query log)

68 items. **20 solved, 5 partly solved, 6 not real defects, 37 pending.**

| area | pending | what they are |
|---|---|---|
| Employee / directory answers | 7 | headings that misstate the question (7), "Understood as" does not name the e-mail source (15), a mobile prefix (17), compound surnames unmeasured (20), dual-surname lists (21), a "Count 5" chip (27), **a wasted 4-6 s people search on every Enter (32)** |
| Platform | 3 | a database outage silently becomes a circular search (28), a 62 s wait before an error (29), rotating secrets (12: you decided it does not matter) |
| Circulars and EDS | 27 | **wrong or invented numbers from badly read scans (51, 54, 55, 56, 63, 65, 66)**, synthesis sees too little text or cites the wrong thing (47-50, 58, 64), synthesis details (38-43, 52, 53), search ranking and confidence (37, 40, 57, 62), smart routing (59, 60), optional (45) |

Since that count (11 to 13 Oct) some of the 27 were closed (OCR corruption fix, archive re-read, health check-up pipeline...)
but **nobody recorded which**. The baseline run (step 2 below) answers this with facts, not memory.

| 33a | **The Gemini reading check of the archive** (`scripts/check-readings.ts`, `src/lib/ingestion/geminiCheck.ts`): for every page the pipeline has read (its reading comes from the re-read tool's page cache, so run the re-read DRY RUN first), Gemini reads the same page image; the two readings are compared number by number (`readingCheck.ts`); a report in `reread-reports/` ranks the pages to look at and links each page image. READ-ONLY: it writes nothing to the database. Gemini's readings are saved in `reread-cache/gemini/` (a page is never paid for twice; an incomplete or failed reading is never saved), calls are spaced (`--pause-ms`, default 6000), a rate-limit refusal waits and retries twice then stops, and `--max-calls` (default 100) caps live calls per run. `--plan` only says how many calls it would make. Tested here against a real local Postgres with a stand-in for Gemini that answers with your real pilot readings (the gate pass pin code 713203 and the telephone numbers were caught; the roster page and a page without a cached reading were skipped; a second run read nothing live; the call limit stopped a third run; the database was unchanged). **Not yet run on your data or with the real Gemini service on archive pages.** Also: the Gemini reader now sends the page image with its real kind (PNG, JPEG or WebP, read from the first bytes) instead of always saying PNG. |
| 34a | **The wage question collapsed without an answer (17 Oct, after the wage notice was re-read and applied).** The terminal said `answer/unverified`: the model wrote an answer, a check refused to prove it, so the card disappeared and only the documents stayed (the designed fallback, but the answer did not appear). The refusal reason was in the API reply but the terminal line cut it at the first colon. **Found by replaying the real stored text:** the rule `columnBesideValue` demanded that the model's WHOLE column label stand within a few characters of the value in the quote. The stored heading is long (`Daily Wages (Rs. Per Day of duty)`); a correct answer is accepted with the full heading and REFUSED with any natural shorter label (`Daily Wages`, `Daily Wages (Rs.)`, `Wages per day`), and our own prompt gives the short example "Wages Per Day". It was not possible to see the model's actual reply, so this is the most likely cause, not a proven one. **Fix:** in a rebuilt table row the value must now sit in a CELL whose label contains every word of the column the model names (it may shorten or reorder a heading), so a value still cannot be passed off under another column's label (a total is not a daily wage; tested). **The refusal reason is now printed in full** as `[answer] not shown: ...` in the terminal. 96 checks (14 new, on the real wage notice text). |

| 73 | **Executive Deep Synthesis: one briefing per question (8 Nov 2026).** The flow chart in Gemini's notes (quality evaluator, score >= 85, persistent store) was NOT in the code: the only mechanism was a per-server memory cache, useless on Vercel (every instance and cold start starts empty) and keyed on the retrieved sources, so the same question gave different briefings (one morning a pie, the next columns). **Now:** (1) `src/lib/search/edsStore.ts` + `20_eds_briefing.sql`: the first GOOD briefing of a question is stored in `public.eds_briefing` (key = sha256 of engine signature + the question without capitals/spaces/punctuation; the signature includes a hash of the prompt, so a changed prompt can never serve an old briefing); every later ask on any server reads it until the archive of circulars changes (fingerprint of circulars + chunks); two servers racing both show the row stored first; a store failure never breaks an answer; the table is created on first use if the DB user may (RLS on, no public API access). (2) `src/lib/search/edsQuality.ts`: plain-code score out of 100 (grounding 30, coverage of the question's words 25, structure 25, chart quality 10, citations 10); 2 candidates are written at once (fixed seeds, temperature 0; `EDS_CANDIDATES` 1-3), the best is kept, one scoring below 60 is shown but never stored (the next ask tries again). | 67d | **Faster saves: the table layout and the code lists are remembered for five minutes (owner, 1 Nov 2026).** Every search, add, change and delete first asked the database what columns the tables have (`discover`) and, for add and change, for the whole list of designations and departments (`loadLookups`): three round trips to Supabase on EVERY save. NOW: remembered for 5 minutes, SEPARATELY (the layout; the lists), on the DATABASE CONNECTION (`db.cacheKey`; the employee manager's own wrapper `typeormEmpDb` in `access.ts` carries the shared connection as its key; a wrapper without a key remembers nothing, as in the tests), never in a global, and only the layout and the codes and titles: nothing about a person. WHY IT CANNOT MAKE A SAVE WRONG: (1) when a panel opens (the `lookups` request) the layout and the lists are read FRESH and remembered, so the screen is never staler than the moment it was opened; (2) a save refused only because a designation or department is not in the REMEMBERED list reads the real list and checks again before giving up (a designation added by a script a minute ago works at once; one that exists nowhere is still refused); (3) a designation or department REMOVED since it was remembered is refused by the database (foreign key): that becomes the message 'The list of designations or departments has just changed: please open the form again and choose once more', the memory is cleared and nothing is half written. MEASURED (queries sent to the database by one save, counted by a wrapper): a CHANGE 11 -> 8, an ADD 18 -> 15; a search and a read lose one. On a machine where a query costs about half a second that is about 1.2 to 1.5 seconds per save: real, but the other 8 to 15 queries remain (the locks, the row, the login account, the history, the name tables). The PAX editor has no layout or code lists to remember (its lines must be read fresh each time, for the clash check), so it is unchanged. PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): the employee store test 131 (18 new: the counts above; the second save reads neither; a wrapper without a key remembers nothing; per database; expiry after 5 minutes and re-remembering; clear; a panel opening reads fresh; a designation added behind its back is accepted; one that exists nowhere is refused; one removed behind its back gives the message and writes nothing; the production wrapper's key) and the memory deliberately broken 5 ways (healing, the foreign-key message, the expiry, the fresh read, the key), each caught. NOT VERIFIED on the live database. | owner to install and test |
| 67c | **Adding an employee on the LIVE roster (owner's log, 1 Nov 2026).** Saving a new employee failed with `null value in column "id" of relation "employee_roster" violates not-null constraint`: the live roster's `id` has NO automatic numbering (its rows were loaded with fixed ids) and the insert never gave one (the test tables had automatic ids, so nothing caught it; the same trap as the designation list in 66c). FIX: when the column is NOT NULL without a default (the store already detects that kind of column), the add takes the NEXT id (highest plus one) itself, with the table LOCKED against every writer (`LOCK TABLE ... SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE`): two adds at once get two different ids, and a SQL script inserting at the same moment makes the add wait and then take the next id after it, so two inserts can never take the same number. A table that numbers its own ids is left alone (no lock, no hand-made id). PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): the employee store test now 113 (5 new, with the table's id default DROPPED, as live: a plain add, two adds at once, an uncommitted script row, the login linked, the default restored); the fix removed makes the run crash with the same error, the lock removed makes the script-collision check fail. NOT VERIFIED on the live table: the add also creates the login account in `public."user"`; if that table has a required column the manager does not fill, the next error will say which (the owner's query for NOT NULL columns without a default shows it beforehand). | owner to install and test |
| 67b | **Two people at once: the guard against a LOST UPDATE, and the name known before anything is asked (owner, 1 Nov 2026: "since several authorised people may change the same employee or the same PAX line we have to guard that robustly"; "get the person's data from the session cookie, no fresh request").** THE PROBLEM: the stores already took a global write lock, which makes two saves at the very same instant safe, but nothing asked whether the form being saved was still what the database held: A changes Ravi's designation, B (whose form is older) fixes only an address, and B's save puts the OLD designation back, silently. Delete had the same hole. Employees and PAX lines both. THE GUARD (`src/lib/concurrency/version.ts`): every record the manager shows carries a VERSION, an HMAC (key: NEXTAUTH_SECRET, the secret NextAuth already needs; with none, a fixed development text) of the RAW values of the fields that can change (the real mobile and e-mails, not the masks: a plain hash of a masked number could be brute-forced). A change or a delete must send the version the person SAW; the store LOCKS THE ROW (`FOR UPDATE`, also the login account's) and works the version out again; if it differs the save is REFUSED as 'stale': nothing is written, the answer carries the record as it is now (as THIS person may see it) and the last change recorded (who, when, what kind; never a value). A missing or made-up version is refused the same way. The row lock also holds back any writer that does not know the global lock (a SQL script): the save waits for it and is then refused as stale, the script's change survives. The token is of the CONTENT, so a change by anything is seen, no new column, and a value changed and put back is correctly not a conflict. An authorised executive's version leaves the dates out, so the HOD changing only a date does not make his open form stale. THE SCREEN: a card 'Somebody else changed this record (line) while you were editing it' says who and when, lists WHAT they changed (it was / it is now), and offers 'Keep my edits on top of their version' (a THREE-WAY MERGE: a field the person changed keeps his value, every other field takes theirs, so he can never put back what they changed; a field both changed is kept as his and named in an amber message) or 'Show their version (drop my edits)'. The save button waits, with the reason, while the card is shown. A stale DELETE re-asks with the record as it is now and says who changed it. THE NAME: it travels WITH the answer that opens the employee manager or the PAX editor (the directory's name for the login's ticket, asked IN PARALLEL with the authority check, so no extra waiting), so the header has it on the very first paint; otherwise the login cookie's session name is used (`src/hooks/useSessionName.ts`: display only, safe without a provider; the owner checked that every login name equals the directory name); only when neither has it is it asked for, and then it is shown as soon as it arrives, never held back by the lookups (before, it waited for BOTH requests). Nothing is DECIDED on the cookie: who may do what is checked by the server, from the database, on every request (a withdrawn authority takes effect at once; a cookie would keep saying 'authorised'). WHAT TO KNOW: production must have NEXTAUTH_SECRET set (it must already, for NextAuth). A panel opened BEFORE this patch carries no version: refresh the page once, or its first save says 'stale'. Add and the same-name check were already safe (serialised by the global lock; now proved by tests). No SQL. PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): version token 13; employee store 108 and PAX store 102, with REAL parallel connections (two saves of one version: one wins; change against delete; five people at once; two adds of the same name or number; a SQL script with an uncommitted change: the save waits then is stale; the real mobile covered; the dates left out for an executive) and the guard deliberately broken 5 ways (employees) and 4 ways (PAX), each caught; form model 34; panel tests 96 and 106; click-throughs of the REAL panels against the real handlers: employee 86 and PAX 66, including HOD against an authorised executive (the card, keep mine, show theirs, an overlap, a stale delete) and the name checks (no 'me' request; the name shown while the lookups are still on their way). NOT VERIFIED: the live database (it needs the audit tables of steps 64 and 66 as they are), the look in the owner's browser. | owner to install and test |

PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): the ORIGINAL 51-step PAX click-through, not modified at all, passes against the new look (add, clash questions, change, delete, authorise, withdraw, history, StrictMode, a real handler and database); the panel test 86 (62 old, two old checks adapted to the new markup: the error colour and the history cells, now read as cells; 24 new); the scale sheet's test 42 and it sees the new file; the REAL components were rendered with the data of the approved pictures and compared with them: four screens are pixel-identical, one differs by 0.14% (the wording of the 'Changing' line), three differ only by the Search button and by the clash card moving above the bar. | 66a | **The employee manager: add, change and delete employees (owner, 1 Nov 2026).** The HOD of C&IT first; he authorises and withdraws executives (a list of its own, `employee_admin_delegate`, apart from the PAX editor's). ASKED FOR in the search bar by a sentence led by an action word ('add new employee', 'edit employee record', 'change dob of X', 'delete employee 498880', 'delegate employee editing to X'); a question about employees is never taken for a change; the person is the LOGIN's ticket, never anything typed. WHAT IT EDITS: the roster row (ticket, SAIL P.No., name, designation, department, position in the grade, the doctor flag, address, two e-mails) and the two dates; NOT the login account's password or role. THE FORM: four sections (Identity, Posting, Service dates, Contact), live validation (the SAME function runs in the panel and on the server), designation and department lists that follow the ticket, a live card of how he will appear, a review step (a new person's summary; a change field by field) and a button that stays off until the record is right. RULES: ticket 6 digits; executive = starts with 4 (the cohort is never typed); 49xxxx = direct recruit; designation and department must fit the cohort; a person goes LAST in his grade (rank = grade order x 10000 + position, the HR formula; a new designation clears the old position); the same name in the same department asks 'is this a different person?'; the ticket cannot be changed (delete and add). DATES stay the HOD's: an authorised executive neither sees nor sets them (a locked card, no date in his searches or in the history, a date he sends is ignored); a date the HOD sets by hand is marked real (hr). DELETE: the HOD only, the ticket typed back, never the HOD himself, the login account removed with him unless unticked; an authorised executive who is deleted loses the authority. HISTORY (`employee_admin_audit`, append only by a trigger): who did what to whom and the record before and after, written in the SAME transaction as the change; the HOD reads dates in it, a delegate reads 'hidden'. The store DISCOVERS the roster's optional columns (a table without address, web-mail or the doctor flag still works; a NOT NULL source_snapshot_date is filled with today). `sql/15_employee_admin.sql` creates the two tables (run once; the panel names the file if it is missing). The date guard now allows exactly THREE readers of the date columns (the lists, the date lookup, this store). PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): intent 62, validation 41, store 68 (a roster with the live rules: unique ticket+cohort, the login link, a NOT NULL snapshot date; concurrency; atomicity; each security rule mutated and the tests fail), search bar 25, panel 44, and a click-through of the REAL panel under StrictMode against the real handler and a database (45 steps, the HOD and a delegate). NOT VERIFIED: the two SQL files (14 re-run, 15) on the live database; the live roster's rules beyond the handoff's description (the four schema queries are still to be run); the look of the form in the real modal. OPEN: photo and mobile number (they live in the login account's table, not the roster); an undo of a delete (the history holds the whole record). | owner to install and test |
| 65c | **Luxon everywhere, realistic sample ages, the roster-reload rule (owner, 1 Nov 2026).** (1) AUDIT: no other date library is imported anywhere (no moment, dayjs, js-joda ...). `date-fns` is declared in package.json but imported by NO file of the program: it is only the required peer of `react-day-picker 8`, a calendar WIDGET used by EventCalendar, YearCalendarModal and CircularUploadModal (Luxon has no calendar widget; each of the three keeps its state in Luxon and makes a Date only at the widget's edge). Both packages STAY (removing date-fns would break the widget). (2) Every place that used the built-in Date for DATE HANDLING (20 files: table-cell dates, the announcement clock, the holiday toast, the PAX history and delegate dates, real-calendar-day checks, the schedule helpers, the year of a circular, the timestamps written into cache and report files) is now Luxon, through the small helpers of `src/lib/dates.ts`. `dates.selftest.ts` keeps the OLD code as a reference and compares over many inputs, run in four time zones; a GUARD fails if native date handling or another date library comes back. The 53 uses of `Date.now()` (stopwatches, cache lifetimes, rate-limit windows, file-name suffixes) are epoch-millisecond arithmetic, not date handling, and are left. TWO DELIBERATE CHANGES: the date beside the announcement clock is the day in INDIA (the old text printed the UTC day: yesterday's date from midnight to 5:30 am), and the PAX history shows India time whatever the visitor's zone. (3) SAMPLE AGES (found in the owner's screenshots of 1 Nov): promoted executives of 19 to 20 (Junior Manager, Asst. Manager grade 201) were impossible, as a promotee is promoted after many years. Now a promoted executive is never younger than 35, a doctor never younger than 25 and joined at 23 or later, a direct recruit 23, a non-executive 18. RUN sql/14 AGAIN to apply (it re-makes every sample date; real dates untouched). (4) The date columns STAY on employee_roster (decided); the rule is written in the SQL: reload the roster by UPSERT on ticket, never empty or re-create the table. | 63a | **The PAX phone-number search (31 Oct 2026; the first feature of the new chat).** PAX = the land-line phone that works only inside DSP work areas (5 digits; the Telecom department keeps the list in `public.pax_directory`, sql/12, live: 65 rows, 54 names). A new deterministic route in `runOmnibar`, FIRST (before the hospital route, because the word "pax" is explicit), no model call, reading the table with the application's OWN connection (`getDb()`), never the model's read-only client (a test checks the wiring; the table is not in the `nlq` schema). New files: `src/lib/pax/matcher.ts` (the planner and the matcher, pure), `answer.ts` (the card: the number or name as the big headline, table, folded "What is PAX?"; reuses `HospitalAnswerCard` through `payload.hospital`, so the screen needs no change), `store.ts` (the loader; the area comes from `public.pax_area()` and its wording is normalised). WHAT IT ANSWERS: name to number ("pax number of fire service", "pax c&it", "pax civil a zone", spelling slips said on screen), number to name ("who has 42046", "pax 420" = prefix, several numbers), lists and counts ("pax", "how many pax numbers", "pax numbers outside plant", "township pax numbers"), faults ("pax not working" goes to the rows named PAX Complain; the MRS Control electrical desk only when asked for). WHEN IT TAKES A QUESTION: the word "pax" always; one bare 5-digit number only if it IS in the directory; a weak cue (extension, intercom, landline, telephone, phone) only on an exact full name match and never for a person, a doctor, the hospital or a fault; never a number of six or more digits or a word about circulars (notice, policy, tariff...). A bare name ("fire service") is NOT taken. NOT FOUND is an amber "Not found" (closest names as a line, never as the answer); a number on two names (42043) and an unclassified number (46298) get a red note. Feedback source is tagged `holiday`, like the hospital's. `nameSearchGate.ts`: "pax" and "extension" are topic words, so the people name search is not fired for them. PROOF (sandbox, NOT live): `pax.selftest.ts` 146 checks, `paxStore.selftest.ts` 9 checks against a real Postgres, `nameSearchGate.selftest.ts` 55; the new checks fail on the old omnibar.ts and gate; type check, lint, big suite 770, 92 other suites, `next build` OK; the install script run twice from the original files. | 62a | **The search bar stopped opening after the first closing (found 31 Oct 2026; it had been there "from early on").** In Chrome with a CLASSIC scrollbar (a Mac with a mouse, or "always show scroll bars"; live site and localhost alike) the omnibar opened once; after closing it with the X a click on the search bar only gave it a blue ring, and the first Ctrl+K did nothing while the second opened it (the open flag was true, the box gone). Cause, found by driving the REAL home page in Chromium with classic scrollbars (a stand-in page and a headless browser without scrollbars never showed it, and my first test did not even check that the box had closed): the scroll lock in OmnibarModal changes the page width by the scrollbar's width when it is set and released; the box's framer-motion `layout` animation then ran against its exit animation and AnimatePresence deleted the NEW box 3 ms after it appeared. Fix: the `layout` prop is removed from the box (src/components/OmnibarModal.tsx, one line plus a comment); the box keeps its fade and slide, only its height no longer glides when an answer arrives. Proof: with classic scrollbars the original fails in round 2, the fixed file reopens in every round, also 40 ms after closing, with and without StrictMode, and the page is released; with the original and overlay scrollbars it never failed, which is why it looked random. New check src/components/OmnibarModal.layout.selftest.ts (5; fails on the old file). No other component has a `layout` prop. | patch_step62a |

| 58b | **Two errors found on the owner's screens of 26 Oct 2026 12:46 to 12:48 (the cards of 57b).** CONFIRMED WORKING ON SCREEN: "specialisation of dr rajeev kumar" (the headline DNB(MEDICINE), one sentence, one bullet), "is dr rajeev kumar a DNB doctor?" (a green Yes), "is dr rajeev kumar a MS doctor?" and "is dr dipak singh a DNB doctor?" (a red No), "list of DNB doctors" (the headline Dr. Rajeev Kumar, one sentence with the gap, the DrNB bullet, the 53 names folded). ERROR 1: "is dr rajeev kumar a MD doctor?" answered with the LIST "17 doctors, Doctors with MD" instead of a red No. CAUSE: "md" (and "dm") is a degree AND an abbreviation that belongs to names ("Md. Anwar"), so it was deliberately NOT among the words that are not names (ms is, mr mrs ms); the engine looked for a doctor with the name word "md", found none, and fell through to the list of MD doctors. FIX (findDoctors): "md" and "dm" are tried as name words first; only when NO doctor has them is the question taken to name the degree and the lookup is repeated without them; so a doctor really named Md ... is still found. Now: "is dr rajeev kumar a MD doctor?" -> No (he holds DNB), "is dr dipak singh a MD doctor?" -> Yes, "is dr rajeev kumar a DM doctor?" -> No; "list of MD doctors" (17), "in medicine" (3) and "doctors with DM" (None) unchanged. ERROR 2: the folded line "How is a specialisation decided?" was shown OPEN. CAUSE (proved in Chromium with the real component): the <details> were keyed by their position only, so React reused the same element for the next answer and a line the person had opened stayed open: old keying: closed at first, opened by the person, OPEN on the next answer (wrong); fixed: closed (right). FIX: HospitalAnswerCard keys each <details> by the answer's title, the position and the summary. Tests: qualification.selftest.ts 130 (5 new for MD / DM; they fail on the 58a engine), HospitalAnswerCard.headline.selftest.tsx 21 (one new: the keying; fails on the 58a card). THIS PATCH IS CUMULATIVE (it carries all of 58a, which carries 57b), so it applies whatever of those is installed. Not verified live. |

FOUND BY THE SUITE: put into DOCTOR_DOC itself (675, then 227 characters) it pushed the doctor prompt over its budget (medicalCadre.selftest: 17,000 characters, only about 60 spare), so DOCTOR_DOC is unchanged and the rule is conditional; isDoctorQuestion also recognises dnb, drnb and mbbs. ANSWER: NO, the data contradicts it. The 66 non-permanent doctors have list numbers D + 6 digits (51; NO qualification recorded for any of them) or DTN + digits (15; 13 with a qualification: 0 DNB, 3 MS, 2 MD, 4 MBBS, 1 MBBS+DGO, 1 MDS, 1 D.Ortho, 1 MCh & DrNB). The one doctor who prints DNB (Rajeev Kumar) has a plain ticket (499169). Our own notes say the letters mark a NON-PERMANENT engagement (the intranet doctor list), not a qualification. Treating them as DNB would put a confident wrong label on 66 people. If the hospital's DNB trainees are in fact given such numbers, that is a different fact ("DNB trainee"), to be recorded as such from a list, never as DNB-qualified. WHAT WOULD COMPLETE IT: the qualifications of the 53 non-permanent doctors (the old intranet doctor page may have them). Tests: qualification.selftest.ts 52 (16 of its checks fail on the old engine, the prompt checks included); engine.ts and medicalCadre.ts replaced whole, prompt.ts changed in place. Not verified live. |

| 55b | **The spelling safety net for name searches: "rupak gosyami" found nobody although "Rupak" was perfect (owner, 26 Oct 2026, after 45 days on phonetic matching; the owner's worry: "do we need 5 million patches?" Answer: no: it is general, and measured).** ROOT CAUSE, PROVED on the scratch directory (6,531 rows, holds Rupak Kumar Goswami): (1) the phonetic CODE treats the "w" of Goswami as a hard consonant (W -> B): GOSWAMI = GASBAMA, but GOSYAMI, GOSAMI, GOSAMY and GOSHAMI = GASAMA, so they never match (only spellings that keep the w do); in Bengali speech that w (and y) after a consonant is silent (Goswami = Goshami; Swapan; Biswas; Dwijen; Sandhya). (2) the deeper gap: a name search is an AND of EXACT phonetic codes, so one word off in one name word drops the person however perfect the other words are; there is no notion of "nearest spelling", and more spelling rules cannot close that (every new way of writing a name is a new rule: the owner's own lesson "enumeration never converges", recorded in the front-path handoff, had not been applied to the name search). Honest note: the one-letter tolerance had been built on 25 Oct only for the DOCTOR search of the hospital route; it was never carried to the employee directory. THE FIX (no database change, NO list of names): when a name search finds NOBODY, `nameSpelling.ts` takes each name word of the model's SQL (nlq.word_like / first_word_like / last_word_like calls and 'X' = ANY(name_words)); a word that no real name is spelled or coded like is replaced by the NEAREST real name word of the directory (the vocabulary is read from nlq.employees.name_words every ten minutes, so a new person is covered at once) and the SAME SQL runs again (same guard and read-only executor), so every other condition of the question stays. How near: a key in which w and y after a consonant and before a vowel are silent, folded like the database folds (foldToken, the JavaScript twin of indic_fold), then edit distance (a swapped pair counts as one, the first two letters swapped too). Accepted: a word that SOUNDS the same under the key, or one slip in the letters (two in a word of 9+ letters) with the same first sound ("swapan" is NOT "tapan"); never for a word of fewer than 4 letters; never for a word that is already known (same word or same code as a real name); two or three equally near names are all searched (OR); more than three equally near: nothing is guessed. A question about people who do not exist stays empty (both words real names: nothing corrected, nobody invented). The answer says so in an amber box above "Departments covered": "No name is spelled “Gosyami” (nearest: Goswami): showing the nearest real spelling instead." and, when a word is nearly as close to other names, "(nearest: Sen; also close: Somen or Soren)"; the SQL shown is the corrected one. MEASURED, as a permanent test (`nameSpelling.coverage.selftest.ts`, 17 s): the 642 REAL name words of the CO & CC and Refractory lists (__fixtures__/name_words.real.json: words only, no tickets, no persons) each broken in every way of nine families of typo, 44,568 broken spellings: the old phonetic rule alone reaches the right name for 58%, with the net 92%; wrongly corrected 223 (0.50%); another 416 (0.9%) sound exactly like a DIFFERENT real name (the old rule finds that person); not found 3,036 (6.8%). By family (old rule alone -> with the net): Bengali glide w/y added or dropped 47% -> 100%; extra vowel / h / w / y / doubled letter 71% -> 98%; a confusable letter (a/o, e/i, o/u, c/k, j/z, b/v, d/t, s/c) 72% -> 99%; swapped neighbouring letters 13% -> 96% (the 4% left are swaps of the first letter); one letter missing 13% -> 86%; h added or dropped 95% -> 98%; long vowel written short 95% -> 100%; TWO slips at once 16% -> 32% (hard by nature); first letter wrong 38% -> 38% (a different sound: by design). The wrong corrections are almost all a 4-letter word that is one slip from SEVERAL real names ("soen": Sen, Soren, Somen): ambiguity in the data that no rule removes, which is why the other close names are shown; a stricter rule for 4-letter words was tried and REJECTED (wrong 0.50% -> 0.45% for a loss of 8 points on a missing letter). The thresholds of the test sit just under these numbers, so a change that makes the net worse fails. Also: unit tests (45), the amber box (4), and eleven end-to-end checks in the big suite on the real database and the real phonetic functions (the exact scenario: gosyami, gosami, goshami, gosamy all find Rupak Kumar Goswami; the right spelling has no note; two wrong words both corrected; sarkar and xyzzyq not invented): 755 checks. Files: nameSpelling.ts (new), pipeline.ts (2 places), types.ts, omnibar.ts, clientTypes.ts, Nl2SqlAnswerExtras.tsx, eval/selftest.ts by cleanup/apply-step55b.js (all-or-nothing, idempotent, independent of step 55a: proved on a copy without it). NOT claimed: 100%. Names written very differently, other word orders and nicknames are not covered; when nothing is near enough the answer stays "0" rather than guess. The primary rule (indic_fold in the live database, which treats the w as a consonant) was NOT changed: its live definition is not in the repository (only the local stand-in) and must be read first (SELECT pg_get_functiondef('public.indic_fold(text)'::regprocedure);); changing it also needs the stored codes refreshed (nlq.refresh_name_codes()) and its JavaScript twin kept identical. Next step: the real misspellings that failed in the office, to add to the tests. Not verified live. |

Nearest: ..."), also on a closed day; not for a doctor who has retired; the old bullet that said the same is dropped; a unit that does not sit also shows the unit that SITS on the asked date. (2) a Sunday is closed EVERY week: "No: The OPD is closed on every Sunday, 25 October 2026: Sunday." (the owner's sentence, kept exactly; also "closed on every Sunday" for a department, a unit, a doctor, the rules and the timings; a holiday that is not a Sunday has no "every"). (3) "is opd open on monday?" has no clear answer: whether YOU can be seen depends on the department and, for a department of units, on the unit you already belong to (a patient is seen only by the unit of the first visit; a new patient belongs to the unit that sits on the day of his first visit). So, when the day is open and the question wants a verdict (starts with is / are / will / does / can ...): an AMBER "Depends" first answer ("The OPD is open on Monday, 26 October 2026 in general ..., but this is not yet a clear Yes: it depends on your department ..."), when the day is (today is Sunday: "The coming Monday is 26 October 2026 (today is Sunday)." / "Today is ..."), in session until ..., the hours, and the QUESTION as the last line of the card: **which department?** (radio, 13 departments, an "I am not sure" that names the general OPD and the Emergency Department). A department of ROOMS then gives a clear Yes or No with its table. A department of UNITS (Medicine) asks **which unit do you already belong to?** (radio: each unit with its days and current doctors, the table of all units with rooms and doctors above it, and "I am a new patient"). A unit gives the clear Yes (green) or No (red, with the banner and the unit that sits that day). A **new patient** gets a clear Yes ("You can visit Medicine OPD on Monday, 26 October 2026. As a new patient you will be seen by Unit II, the unit that sits that day, and you will belong to it from then on."), a banner ("Your first visit decides your unit ... Know the other units before you go."), the unit that sits that day with rooms and doctors, and ALL units with days, rooms and doctors. A closed day, an unknown holiday, or today out of hours is answered at once (no question): a clear No / amber Not known. A STATEMENT ("opd open on monday", "medicine opd monday") is not a question and still gets the plain answer and the table. The rewriting is the 54a mechanism (the person's own question, with the chosen department or unit put in the place of the word OPD; a new patient is the marker "(new patient)"); the round trip runs every department and every unit. | 51a | **The hospital OPD route (a new deterministic route in the omnibar, no model call).** The owner's brief (25 Oct 2026): the hospital has OPD, inpatient, test lab, X-ray, CT scan, ultrasound/USG, colour Doppler, TMT, ECG, echocardiogram, pathology, endoscopy, colonoscopy, ENT and eye tests; in OPD a department's doctors are split into units, the first-named doctor of a unit is its head, one of the unit heads is the department's HOD (mostly unknown), a patient allotted to a unit consults only that unit; OPD is closed on Sundays, the 4 national holidays, SAIL Day, Ambedkar's birthday, 25 December and the puja holidays (list not complete); the lists given are not up to date and no test data exists yet, so data is added incrementally. **Built on the data in hand:** `src/lib/hospital/data/hospital_staff.json` (102 people: 92 doctors + 10 other staff, from the staff list and the OPD schedule, linked to the intranet doctor list: 62 current, 27 not_on_list (unconfirmed), retired: Ranjeet Kumar, Ivy Paul, transferred: Dadan Prasad (told by the owner)) and `data/opd_schedule.json` (13 departments, 25 OPD units, every weekday cell: doctors or a state OT_DAY / CLOSED / ANY_AVAILABLE / PROCEDURE_EMERGENCY / UNKNOWN for a blank cell; timings OPD 8:00-13:00 and 17:00-19:00, token 8:00-11:30 and 16:30-18:30; the closure rules). **Closure rules:** Sundays; fixed dates (24 Jan SAIL Foundation Day, 26 Jan, 1 May, 15 Aug, 2 Oct, 14 Apr Ambedkar, 25 Dec); the plant's CH holidays and every FH holiday whose name contains "puja" from the holiday data of that year; any OTHER plant holiday is reported as NOT KNOWN (neither open nor closed); a year not loaded says so. **Understood:** open/closed today / tomorrow / weekday / date (with the reason, past tense for past dates), a doctor's OPD days and whether he sits on a date, a department's OPD on a weekday or date (also two departments at once), a unit by OPD number / clinic number / name (Kanishka, Joydeb) / "unit 2" of a department, a department at a glance, the head of a unit and the HOD, timings, the closure rules, the list of OPDs; ambiguous names ("mukherjee", "das") ask for the first name; a name nobody has is said so. Retired / transferred / not-on-list doctors are MARKED in every answer and a slot whose doctors are all gone gets a note. **Gating:** needs the word OPD (or an OPD number, a unit name, a doctor + days words, "hod"/"unit head" + a department, "token timings"); document words (mediclaim, circular, policy ...) and employee words (ticket, designation, senior, how many ...) leave the question to the other routes; 16 such questions are tested as left alone. **Wiring:** `omnibar.ts` runs it BEFORE the holiday parser (step 0; so "is opd open on 25 december" is not taken by the holiday parser), skipped on a retry, optional dependency `deps.hospital`; payload field `hospital` (types in analytics.ts + useOmniSearch.ts, kept in sync); the card `HospitalAnswerCard.tsx` drawn first in OmnibarModal; `holidaysOn.ts` reads the holiday tables (`queryHolidays`). The time: each model call is about 5,000-6,000 tokens and Groq's free per-minute token limit made the 2nd call wait 23 s and the 3rd 45 s (the log: "waited 69.0s for Groq's per-minute limit"; the same question had answered correctly, 63 executives, at 14:58 with 2 calls: the model is not deterministic). **Fix: `repairFamilies` in `familyCheck.ts`**: when the question names exactly ONE family and the SQL covers it wrongly, the first `department_id IN (...)` or `department_code IN (...)` (a list or a sub-query, the end found by matching brackets, quotes respected) becomes `department_id IN (the family's ids)`; the mended SQL is returned only if it passes `checkFamilies` again, and `pipeline.ts` re-runs the real guard on it before using it (a new block after the safety guard: `g.sql = g2.sql`; the model is asked again only if it cannot be mended). **Not mended (sent back to the model as before):** two or more families named (a comparison: the model's own grouping would be lost); no `department_id IN`/`department_code IN` to put the ids into; **a question that names ONE SECTION** ("BLAST FURNACE (OPERATION)", "main hospital office", "sms ccp") because widening it to the whole department would be a wrong answer: **the existing 734-check suite caught exactly this in the first version** (the "number of people working in BLAST FURNACE (OPERATION)" case was mended to all 12 Blast Furnace rows in one attempt instead of being corrected on attempt 2); the section words are the row words that are not the family's own nicknames (and not a short list of stop words: and, of, the, in, at, for, to, a, an, on, by, with, or, dept, deptt, department(s), section(s)): the same list now also stops "and" from making a part of a family acceptable in `checkFamilies`. The retry message says in so many words when row ids were written as codes ("the numbers you put in code IN (...) are department IDs (the row numbers), not department codes: use them as department_id IN (...)"). **Tests:** `familyCheck.selftest.ts` replaced, 43 checks (25 old + 18 new: the live SQL of 17:41, the LIKE '%hospital%', LIKE '%hydraulic lab%' and LIKE '%l d%' of 14:58 mended, one row of cocc mended, department_code mended with the column changed, already-right SQL untouched, two families / no department condition / no family / unbalanced bracket / tricky brackets and quotes, named sections not widened, "and" not a section); the real pipeline with a stand-in model that has ONE reply only (so a second call would fail the script): ids written as codes -> 1 model call, `department_id IN (10)` in the test database; `LIKE '%hospital%'` -> 1 call; two departments -> 2 calls (sent back once); already right -> untouched; the 734-check suite passes (733 + the Blast Furnace case that the first version broke). **Not verified on the live app** (it needs the model to make the same mistake again). **Known:** the 3 Groq calls and the 80 s were the cost of the mistake; the prompt of about 5,000 tokens per call (the department block, the designations) is the next lever for speed. |

(4) A HINDI WORD read as digits: circular 22 `31 मार्च` -> `3191`, `12 मई` -> `1208`; (5) a merged serial-number column read as ONE number: circular 29 `56789`. **Circular 20's `24000` was `2400/-` (the Pragya course, over 70%) with the `/-` read as a zero (I had said it was invented from Devanagari numerals: wrong).** In circular 33 the age limits 45, 46, 60 vanish from the Hindi page text (they stay on its English page 3): small numerals in Hindi table rows that the English pass read with low confidence over Indic boxes are cut; harmless in a bilingual circular, a known limit for a Hindi-only one. **Root cause of classes 1, 2, 4, reproduced on the real GPAIS Hindi table cell `[का @ 10%]`:** for a table cell that mixes Hindi words and numbers the English reading is not numeric-like and has low confidence, so the cell was read again with the Hindi+Bengali model and that reading REPLACED the English one whole if more confident; the Hindi+Bengali model damages Latin digits next to Indic words (English reading `[iw @ 10%]` conf 57, Hindi reading `[। का @ 0%]` conf 68: the Hindi reading won), and the English model makes digits out of Indic letters. **The fix (`ruledTable.ts`, OCR_MERGE_VERSION 7):** a mixed cell takes its WORDS from the Hindi/Bengali reader and each of its Latin NUMBERS is settled against the numbers the English reader saw at three scales: a number two or more English readings also have stays; a lost leading 1 is put back when the English readings have "1" + the number (`0%` -> `10%`, `000/-` -> `1000/-`); one digit read differently is put right only when ALL THREE English readings are alike (`7600` -> `1600`); anything else is `[unclear]`, never a wrong digit. In a table that holds Indic words, a number-looking cell the Hindi reader sees with a word (`31 मार्च` read as `3191`) must agree with the English reading on the number, or it is `[unclear]` (needs a Hindi reading with confidence 75 or more and 3 or more Indic letters; a Hindi word with no number of its own changes nothing). A Hindi reading that agrees with the English one costs no extra reading. 29 new checks (`ruledTableMixed.selftest.ts`, scripted readers, including the exact three English readings and the Hindi reading the real engine gave for the GPAIS cell); the 85 existing table checks pass. **Real engine, GPAIS Hindi page: the service-charge cell now reads `सेवा शुल्क [। का @ 10%] = (iil)`; all 15 numeric cells and the other cells are identical to before, none unclear, 4.1 s.** **NOT yet verified on the real pages** (needs the PDFs of circulars 20, 22, 31 and 1): the prize tables of circulars 20 and 31, the Hindi date cells of circular 22, the ordinal cases. **NOT yet handled:** class 3 (ordinal letters read as digits: `227`, `215%`, `67`, `15%)`) and class 5 (a merged serial column read as one number). The code had no systematic treatment (a unit label, a currency-word set, one prompt line). **Built (stage 1):** `currency.ts` (SLOT_DIGITS 2/3/7; SLOT_SYMBOLS % ¥ € ®; `attachedDigitSuspects`: an amount that is an amount THE SAME PAGE also states, with one more digit 2, 3 or 7 in front, the shorter having 4 or more digits and standing at least as often, never a year, never a reference number; `rupeeSlotJunk`: a symbol that cannot belong before an amount, right before one; `slotChecks`/`slotChecksFor`: the suspect becomes UNCONFIRMED, reader `rupee-slot`), merged with the twin and second-reading checks (`combineChecks` now names the incoming reader; what two pages agree on, or Gemini confirmed, lifts the suspicion); at UPLOAD (`apply-step42b.js`, route.ts) and in the ARCHIVE (`crosscheck-report.ts`, report-only by default, `--apply` stores); the second reader's prompt (`PAGE_READER_PROMPT`) now says the sign is a currency mark, never a digit, to copy it as printed and never to write it as 2, 3, 7, %, Z or T (this changes the fingerprint under which Gemini readings are cached: earlier readings are read again); the synthesis prompt says the same and what to report for a stray leading digit. **Verified:** on the 11 hard pilot pages in both readings the ONLY amount flagged is the REAL misread of the gift-card Hindi page's Tesseract reading (`(215,000)` where the hand-made truth says 15,000; Gemini's reading of it is not flagged): no false alarm elsewhere; through the answer card's real gate, on a single-page circular, 215,000 is refused and Rs. 15,000 passes; 35 checks (rupee) + 60 (cross-check); install scripts proven on pristine copies, a failed run changes nothing; the archive script run end to end (the real gift-card Hindi page as a single-page circular is recorded with 215000 unconfirmed; a second `--apply` changes nothing). **Known limits:** the text-only check cannot know the truth (a page may state both 15,000 and 215,000 for real: rare, and it only refuses an answer, it never shows a wrong one); a stray digit with no shorter twin on the page is invisible to it. **STAGE 2, not built: a glyph reader** (the real fix at the OCR layer, independent of Gemini and twins). Prototype on synthetic renders: a small classifier on the glyph's shape found the rupee sign in 83% of cases with a WHOLE FONT FAMILY held out (Poppins 1 of 12, FreeSerif 7 of 12 are the weak ones; Carlito, DejaVu Sans, FreeSans 12 of 12) and wrongly called 1 of 1,248 digits or symbols a rupee sign (0.08%); the owner's reference image, never seen in training, scored 0.97 (0.955 shrunk and degraded). It must be trained on the circulars' own fonts: it needs real crops (the gift-card PDFs `09_2026.pdf` / `10_2026.pdf`, the wage notice and any other circular with ₹). A second lead to test on real scans: the `ben` model as a second reader of amount lines. Not tested: how the search box treats a typed ₹ (only the answer card was tested). |
| 42a | **The 40a report was run on the real archive (19 Oct, 49 circulars): 33 have two or more pages, 21 pairs of an Indic page and a Latin page were found, 7 fully clean, 1 with a number read differently (circular 30: Indic `5.2024.2039` against Latin `2024.2039`), 13 with numbers on one page only.** My reading of the 37 numbers listed (from the context snippets only, not the page images): about 17 garbled digits on Hindi pages, 8 clause numbers (2.3, 4.91), 3 digits read from handwritten signatures, 5 reference numbers with junk in front, 2 values missing on one page, 1 handwritten date added by the Gemini reading; almost all the garbage on the Indic pages, about 4 of 37 on English pages. **The important one: circular 51 (the gift card), Hindi page `(215,000)` for Rs 15,000 (the rupee sign read as a 2)**, the error that produced the invented "275,000". **The owner's requirement: circulars come in English and Hindi, and a few also in Bengali, so the system must be trilingual, not bilingual** (the OCR already reads Bengali: the Indic pass is `hin+eng+ben`). **Built: `crossCheck.ts` rewritten.** Scripts are Latin / Devanagari / Bengali (`scriptOf`); pages in different scripts with the same important numbers (at least 2 in common and at least half of their union) are joined into a GROUP of 2 or 3 pages (a page joins through any page it matches, so Hindi and Bengali pages that share too little with each other still join through the English page). In a group: a number on this page and another page is CONFIRMED; a number on this page only that is one or two digits from a number another page has and this page lacks is a DISAGREEMENT (one page against one, or one against two that agree: `support`); a number on this page only that is a longer form of a number the page has elsewhere and another page confirms is a VARIANT (`215000` next to `15000`); anything else on one page only is a SINGLE (shown in the report, NEVER recorded). Clause numbers (2.3, 4.91) and bare years 1990-2039 are not worth concluding about when single; handwritten spans of the second reading are not part of the page. **What is recorded** (the checks the answer card's gate reads; the gate REFUSES an answer whose value holds a number in the page's unconfirmed list, and a confirmed number is exempt): per page the confirmed numbers, and as UNCONFIRMED: both sides of a one-against-one disagreement (nothing says which is wrong: the owner's rule is an honest not-found over a confident wrong answer; English OCR does read numbers far better, so `TRUST_ENGLISH` is a one-line constant, false, that keeps a lone English reading), the lone page when two pages agree against it, and a variant. `combineChecks` merges with the second reading's checks: what Gemini confirmed is never left unconfirmed; `mergeIntoStored` does it for the archive and replaces a stored check about OTHER text (fingerprint differs). **Two ways it is applied:** (1) the ARCHIVE: `scripts/crosscheck-report.ts` is REPORT ONLY by default; `--apply` stores the checks (needs the `circular_page_check` table: SQL 10), merges with existing checks, is safe to repeat, prints the pages now carrying unconfirmed readings; (2) at UPLOAD: `cleanup/apply-step42a.js` (2 edits in `api/circulars/route.ts`, all-or-nothing, idempotent, proven on a pristine copy of the file) stores the twin checks merged with the second reading's, so a new circular is protected even when Gemini is off or failed (`detail.twinPages` in the ingest log). **Verified:** 55 checks on the REAL GPAIS pair and mediclaim Hindi page, constructed three-script sets and the gift-card pattern, including the answer card's real gate (`215,000` refused as not confirmed by the second reading of page 1, `Rs. 15,000` from the English page passes); the script run end to end on a local database (report-only writes nothing; `--apply` stores 7 of 7, keeps and merges a fresh second-reading check, replaces a stale one, a second run changes nothing, `loadPageChecks` reads the rows as fresh, a missing table is explained). **NOT verified: any real Bengali circular** (the Bengali pages of the tests are constructed: whether the real `hin+eng+ben` reading of a Bengali page is good enough to be a second reading is not known; the first report had no Bengali group). **Known limits:** it needs a twin page (21 of 49 had one, a lower bound because garbled Hindi can hide a twin; the 16 single-page circulars and those with no twin still need Gemini); it points at a disagreement, it cannot say which page is right; `scripts/check-readings.ts --save` upserts a page's row and so overwrites the twin check of that page: run `crosscheck-report.ts --apply` again after it. **Still to do: run `--apply` on the live archive, then the Gemini replacements (gift-card pair, wage notice, GPAIS) when the quota is back.** |

| 36a | **Ending the loop: "are we patching per failed search?" (18 Oct).** Honest answer: partly yes. 34a, 34b and 35b were each a general rule, but the METHOD was reactive, and the cause is structural: the answer card verified TEXT THE MODEL WROTE, word for word, so every reasonable variation of the model's quoting (cells left out, a clause, a shortened heading, a sentence subject stated earlier) could refuse a correct answer, and only one new variation was found per query. The verifier has 17 refusal rules: about 9 protect correctness (value in its quote, label tied to the same table row, column beside the value, numbers of the heading in a quote, "daily" backed by a column, the confirmation gate ...) and about 8 only guarded PRESENTATION or the model's copying (heading over 90 characters, note over 220, more than 12 rows, quote over 300, label over 60, quote not word for word ...). **The change (generic, in `circularAnswer.ts`):** (1) **Verify the fact, not the wording.** When a quote is not a copy of the document, too long, or does not hold the value, the system finds the VALUE in the document (a number is never found inside a longer one: `15,000` is not in `215,000` or `1,15,000`) and takes the document's own words around it: in a rebuilt table, the cells of the value's ROW (row boundaries are where the next row's first cell is glued on without a pipe, or a line starts), with the label required to be a cell of THAT row; in prose, the sentence. The model's quote is accepted as a POINTER to that place only if at least half of its own words stand in those surroundings. The card then shows the document's words (word for word by construction), not the model's. (2) **Presentation is repaired, not refused:** a long heading or label is shortened, a long note is cut at a sentence end or dropped, rows beyond 12 are cut with a note, a value may be up to 120 characters. All correctness rules stay; the change only removes refusals of correct answers (except the fix below, which adds one). **A HOLE THAT 34b OPENED, found by the new combination tests, closed here:** a model could quote a true two-cell piece of the PROJECTS row (`Area: PROJECTS | Daily Wages: 740`) and label it "Works"; the quote has one number, so it was not treated as a table row and its label was never compared with it, so the card would have shown the FALSE fact "Works: 740" (12 of 90 false claims got through). Any quote with table cell separators must now contain the label, and a sentence that merely holds five numbers is no longer mistaken for a table (table-ness is structure or number density). **Tests (149):** every combination of 6 quote styles x 4 column wordings over the real wage table: all 72 true facts accepted, all 90 false claims refused (another row's wage, a total, a basic pay, a value that is not there); the real gift-card corrigendum: all 24 true combinations accepted, false claims (an amount that is not there, a column from another sentence) refused; three tests whose premise was "a quote that is not a copy must be refused" now state "the fact must be true". **Still not measured:** the real model on the whole question set; the answer stage is not in the acceptance harness yet (it only calls the search stage), so the answer card's acceptance rate over the 80 circular questions is unknown. |
| 35b | **The gift-card question collapsed (18 Oct): the model's answer was RIGHT and a rule of mine refused it.** Asked "employees can choose a gift card online, what amount was first announced and what was it corrected to", the search found only the corrigendum (circular 49, 91%), and the terminal printed `[answer] not shown: unverified: the column "Gift Card Amount" is not in its quote` with the model's reply: rows `Initial announced amount` = `Rs.15,000/-` and `Corrected amount` = `Rs.14,999/-`, each with a word-for-word quote. **Cause:** for a sentence (not a table row) the column's words had to be inside the QUOTE itself; the second quote is a clause (`may please be read as "Rs.14,999/-"`) whose subject ("the amount of the Gift Card") stands earlier in the same sentence. Replayed here on the real corrigendum text in its stored form: refused exactly as in your terminal. **Fix:** the column's words may also come from THE SENTENCE of the document that holds the quote (back to the previous full stop followed by a space or line break, forward to the next, at most 400 characters either way). **Only prose qualifies:** a real sentence boundary must be found on both sides, and the window must hold no table cell separator `|` or table marker. This restriction was needed: my first version let a flattened table count as one long sentence and ACCEPTED a total (20995.30) as a daily wage, which an existing safety test caught before delivery. Tested on the real corrigendum: the model's real reply is accepted; with the gate it is accepted when 15000 and 14999 are confirmed and refused when 14999 is only the pipeline's; a column that is not in the sentence (`Daily Wage`), or only in the NEXT sentence (`Last date`), is still refused. 137 checks (21 new). **The correct answer to the question** (from Gemini's reading of the pilot pages; confirm on the page images): first announced Rs 15,000 (circular dated 03.08.2026), corrected to Rs 14,999 by the corrigendum dated 08.08.2026; last date 15.08.2026 unchanged. |
| 35a | **The confirmation gate: the answer card refuses a number that the second reading did not confirm.** Why: the reading check found `215000` in the stored text of circular 51 (the rupee sign read as a digit: `(Rs 15,000)` became `(215,000)`), a WRONG amount that the re-read tool cannot fix (it compares the pipeline with itself) and that an answer could quote. **What was built:** (1) a new table `circular_page_check` (SQL file `src/lib/nl2sql/sql/10_circular_page_check.sql`, run in Supabase one section at a time): per checked page, the numbers both readings had (`confirmed`), the numbers only the pipeline had (`unconfirmed`), the numbers only the second reader had, the reader, a FINGERPRINT of the page's stored text, the time; (2) `scripts/check-readings.ts --save` stores the checks, and ONLY for a page whose stored text is exactly the text that was checked (otherwise it says to run the re-read apply first); a reading that stopped early confirms what it covers and marks nothing unconfirmed; a page whose second reading was unusable is not saved; (3) `src/lib/search/pageChecks.ts` loads the checks of a circular for the answer route and uses a check ONLY while the stored page text still has the saved fingerprint (a re-read circular's old check is stale and ignored: a check can never vouch for text it did not see), and never throws (no table yet, or any database trouble, means no check, exactly as before); (4) the gate in `circularAnswer.ts`: for a checked page, a number of the answer's VALUE that only the pipeline had is refused (`unverified: the value "215,000" is not confirmed by the second reading of page 1`); a number both readings had, a page never checked, and a value with no digits are not affected; the page that holds the quote decides (its longest piece, when the quote leaves cells out); when a row was cut by a chunk break and the quote is in no single part, every page counts and a number is unconfirmed only if NO page confirms it; (5) `cleanup/apply-step35a.js` edits `answer/route.ts` in place (fails closed, idempotent). **Proven here against a real local Postgres:** created the table from the SQL file; `check-readings --save` stored 3 page checks (circular 51 unconfirmed `{215000}`, circular 82 unconfirmed `{9022}`); with those database rows the gate REFUSED `215,000`, ACCEPTED `15,000` and the date `20.08.2026`, ACCEPTED the three wage rows (808, 740, 809 are all confirmed); after the page's stored text changed the check was ignored; `--save` refused to store a page whose stored text was not the checked text. 127 + 15 checks. **DONE on your data (18 Oct):** the SQL was run in Supabase (section 1 `table_exists` empty, section 2 success, section 3 nine columns), 35a applied (route edited, `git status` as expected, `tsc` clean), `check-readings.ts --ids 49,51,82 --save` printed `Saved 4 page check(s)` (no page held back: the stored text of 49, 51 and 82 is the text that was checked), and the wage question still shows 808, 740 and 809 with the gate on. The gate has not yet REFUSED anything on real data: the wage numbers are all confirmed. Note that the gate only acts on pages whose stored text equals the checked text: circulars not yet re-read with the new pipeline must be re-read (apply) first, then checked, then saved. |
| 34b | **The wage question STILL collapsed after 34a: the real refusal (18 Oct).** 34a printed the reason and it was NOT the column rule I had guessed: `[answer] not shown: unverified: a quote is not in the document word for word`. (34a's column rule is still sound and stays: a model may shorten a heading, as long as every word it names is in the label of the same cell as the value.) **What the model is shown (replayed here with the real chunker):** the re-read stores page text with ALL whitespace collapsed (so the table rows are glued together: `... : 808 SL.: 2 | Area: PROJECTS | ...`), cut into 200-word chunks (one chunk break falls inside row 1, between `HRA:` and `1720.30`), joined for the model with a blank line. **Replayed quotes:** the whole row copied exactly is accepted; a quote that LEAVES CELLS OUT (`Area: WORKS | Daily Wages (Rs. Per Day of duty): 808`, or with an ellipsis, or the heading plus selected cells) was REFUSED. A model naturally leaves cells out when asked for "the label, the column and the value together", so this is the most likely cause, but **the model's actual reply was not visible: not proven.** **Fix:** a quote made of pieces (split at `|` and at an ellipsis) is accepted when every piece is word for word in the document, IN ORDER, within ONE table row: the span must be short (at most 450 characters or 3 times the quote) and must not hold the same column label twice (that would be two rows). Refused and tested on the real stored form: pieces from two rows (WORKS with PROJECTS's wage, PROJECTS with the row before or after), an invented number, pieces in the wrong order, a cell that is not there, a paraphrase, a total passed off as a daily wage, a made-up join of the heading with another row. **Diagnostics:** on any refusal the terminal now prints the reason with where the quote stops matching the document, and the model's own reply (first 1500 characters). 116 checks (34 new). **CONFIRMED by you on 18 Oct (screenshots):** the wage card shows Works 808, Projects 740 and Town Services (Elect) 809 under `Daily Wages (Rs. Per Day of duty)`, with the source line `Notice for Contract Workers ... Page 1`; the fold-out of exact words shows the model's own quotes WITH an ellipsis (`SL.: 1 | Area: WORKS ... Daily Wages (Rs. Per Day of duty): 808`), which proves that the cause was a quote that left cells out. Terminal: `answer/answered 5.1s`, 3,619 tokens, no `[answer] not shown` line. The long-pending "answer first on a valid reading" item is closed for the wage notice. |

Re-running the check is free: Gemini's readings are saved. **FIRST REAL RUN (your data, 17 Oct, circulars 49, 51, 82):** the re-read DRY RUN says 49 and 51 are UNCHANGED, and 82 (the wage notice) WOULD CHANGE: the damaged numbers 27000, 000 and 1924530 disappear and the right ones appear (808, 740, 809, 19245.30, 0.00); text 2917 to 3369 chars. The Gemini check read 4 pages: circular 49 AGREED (8 numbers confirmed); circular 51 page 1 DIFFERENCES (only the pipeline has 9.1 and 215000), page 2 DIFFERENCES (only the pipeline has 1816); circular 82 DIFFERENCES (only the pipeline has 9022; the three daily wages are confirmed by both readers); 57 numbers confirmed by both readers; nothing written to the database. **215000 is very likely the rupee sign read as a digit: "(₹ 15,000)" became "(215,000)"** (the pilot's Hindi gift-card page had exactly this, Gemini read "(₹ 15,000)" correctly, and the headline of circular 51 says Rs. 15000); the stored text of circular 51 therefore probably holds a WRONG amount that the re-read cannot fix (it says UNCHANGED): only the second reader finds it. The rupee sign is read as "2", "%" or "€" by Tesseract in other places too (ledger 54).
