## Direction: EDS will cover every data domain, not only circulars (decided with you, 13 Oct)

EDS is the data-mining route for department executives: it must relate facts that look unrelated, in whatever data the plant holds.
Today it reads the **circulars**. Planned domains, added as the data reaches the database: **employee data** (HR executives),
**holidays** (personnel executives), **production**, **tenders**, **payroll**, **medical**, **procurement / purchase orders**.

What this means for the design (nothing below is built yet; it is the target the current work must not block):

- **A domain is a module**: its read-only database views, its vocabulary for the question detector, its own verified facts to test against.
- **Access is decided on the server, per domain, by the executive's department** (HR executives see employee data, personnel executives
  holidays ...). Payroll and medical are sensitive: **default deny**, never "every executive sees everything". The browser never decides this.
- **One engine, several domains**: a question that spans domains needs every domain the person is allowed to see, and says plainly which
  domain a fact came from.
- **Two limits of today are deliberate and temporary**: the detector treats people-data questions as "never EDS" (right while EDS only reads
  circulars; once an employee-data domain exists, an HR executive's complex people question becomes an EDS question for that domain), and
  `/api/ai-search` has no notion of an executive's department yet.

## OCR at the root (17 Oct): causes, what was built, what is still open

**Causes found by experiment, not by guessing.** (1) Tesseract decides the layout of a page by itself; on a page holding a table drawn with lines that decision is unstable: the same engine, on the same scan, dropped the narrow last column, or turned grid lines into "]", and gave a DIFFERENT wrong result at each page scale; read cell by cell it got all 33 cells of the wage table right at five scales. (2) A page tilted by a fraction of a degree makes every table line drift sideways, so the lines break and no grid can be found (the nodal officers scan is tilted). Not causes: the scan's resolution (300 dpi, clean), the colour format.

**Built (step 30b).** `ruledTable.ts`: straighten a tilted page; find the grid from the long lines (merged headings included); read every cell on its own, numbers three times with a vote, "[unclear]" if the readings disagree; rebuild "Column: value | Column: value" rows; inside a table's box the table reading is the ONLY source of numbers (every page word that holds a digit there is cut, and grid-line debris), while every other word STAYS (the page reading often reads a name or title better than a small cell: on the nodal officers page cutting matched words broke two names); the page is always read from the ORIGINAL picture (turning a page resamples it, and a turned page read worse than the original on a real circular; the table boxes are mapped back to the original). **A table reading is used only if it keeps most of the confidently read words that the ordinary page reading saw inside the table's box** (found necessary on 17 Oct: on two pages the first version replaced a good page reading with a poor table reading and lost real words); otherwise that table is left to the page reading, exactly as before. Tables whose data rows are not separated by lines are also left to the page reading (cutting them into cells would glue their rows together). A grid line must be nearly continuous (60% of the table's height), so a tall letter stroke or the aligned first letters of a column of words cannot become a false column boundary (it had cut "Doljatra" into "Dol | atra"). Doubtful text cells (Hindi read as English gives gibberish) are read again by the Hindi/Bengali reader. `pageReader.ts`: one small interface for a second reader, with Gemini behind it, chosen by `PAGE_READER=gemini`. `ocrScore.ts` and `scripts/ocr-pilot.ts`: score every reader against `eval/ocr-pilot-truth.json` (11 hard pages, 120 numbers, 69 phrases, 27 label-with-value pairs, read by eye).

**PILOT RESULT (your run of 17 Oct, 11 hard pages, 120 key numbers, 69 phrases, 26 label-with-value pairs).** Tesseract as before: numbers 86/120 (72%), phrases 55/69, pairs 11/26 (42%), 4 wrong readings, 10 grid-line garbage. New pipeline: numbers 117/120 (97.5%), phrases 64/69, pairs 24/26 (92%), 2 wrong readings, 2 garbage; about one second slower per page. Biggest gains: the GPAIS English table 5/16 to 16/16 numbers, the Hindi GPAIS page 3/16 to 16/16 (the Hindi fallback works on your machine), the wage notice 17/22 to 22/22, the award schemes 4/9 to 9/9 phrases. Gemini was not measured: its model name (gemini-2.5-flash) is retired for new users and Google's own message names gemini-3.8-flash, now the default (override with GEMINI_VISION_MODEL). What the new pipeline still gets wrong, all on pages WITHOUT a table (so the table reader cannot help): the football circular's time ("10 00 AM" read as "0 00 AM") and "5th"; the Hindi mediclaim circular's dates (20-08-2026, 2026/52); a Hindi gift-card line where "गिफ्ट कार्ड" and "15,000" fall on different lines; a printed signature name. These are the cases for the second reader and the agreement check. Three faults the pilot showed in the pipeline itself, fixed in 30c: a garbled leftover number ("1924530", the damaged 19245.30) stayed in the page text; two names on the nodal officers page were broken by cutting words the cell reading also had; the Gemini model name.

**PILOT 2 (17 Oct, with Gemini).** Same 11 pages. Tesseract as before: numbers 86/120, phrases 55/69, pairs 11/26, 3 real wrong readings, 5.0 s per page. New pipeline: 117/120, 66/69, 24/26, 0 real wrong readings, 6.0 s. **Gemini (gemini-3.8-flash): 120/120, 69/69, 26/26, 0 real wrong readings, 20.8 s per page.** Where the pipeline and Gemini differ on the key numbers: never the pipeline alone (0 cases); Gemini alone in 3 (the football "5th", the Hindi mediclaim dates 20-08-2026 and 2026/52, which Tesseract reads as garbled Devanagari digits). So on these pages the two readers never contradicted each other, and Gemini filled every gap. **Correction:** the "wrong reading" counted against all three readers on the football page was a flaw of my truth file (the forbidden text "0 00 AM sharp" is inside the correct "10 00 AM sharp"); all three read "10 00 AM sharp" correctly. The truth file is fixed. The local-model route was measured next and then set aside (see the DECISION below). Local-model test, first attempt (17 Oct): Ollama 0.40.5 reinstalled on the 8 GB MacBook Air, qwen2.5vl:3b pulled (3.2 GB); the first page was refused after 7 s because Ollama's default working memory (4096 tokens) is smaller than one page plus the instructions (4272): the reader now asks for 8192 and caps the reply (30e). **Local model result (qwen2.5vl:3b via Ollama on the 8 GB MacBook Air, no internet, 17 Oct): 10 of 11 pages read; on those 10 pages numbers 104/104, phrases 62/63, label-with-value pairs 24/24, no wrong readings, 76 to 164 s per page (mean 131 s).** The same score as Gemini on those pages (the one phrase missed is a printed signature name). The 11th page (the dense Hindi GPAIS table) failed after 302 s with "fetch failed": Node's fetch gives up if the reply has not STARTED within 300 s, which a laptop-sized model needed for that page; the local reader now streams its reply (30f). One mistake the score cannot see: the model wrote two Cyrillic letters inside a Latin name ("WONМИ HORAM" for "WONMI HORAM"), a known weakness of vision models: a check for letters from a script that does not belong must be part of the reading check. A sample of 10 pages is encouraging, not proof. **Local model on the dense Hindi GPAIS page (streamed, 310 s):** numbers 15/16, phrases 3/6, label+value pairs 0/2. It read the table's numbers correctly, then wrote the blank Excel form under it as 154 blank table rows until the reply cap, so everything after the form (the approval sentence, the designation, the long reference number) was lost; it also misread the label "प्रीमियम" as "प्रतिमय". The pipeline and Gemini read this page completely. So a small local model can be wrong in a way that LOOKS complete: its reading must be checked, and a reading that loops or stops early must count as incomplete. **DECISION (17 Oct 2026, by the user): the local model is set aside; Gemini is the second reader, with the pipeline and the reading check built around it.** Reasons, from the measurements: the small local model (qwen2.5vl:3b) equalled Gemini on 10 pages, but on the dense Hindi page it looped on a blank form and lost the end of the page, and it put Cyrillic letters into a Latin name; it was slow (76 to 310 s per page on an 8 GB laptop). Its code (ollamaReader, PAGE_READER=ollama, `--ollama-model`) was removed in step 31b; the project's own older Ollama fallbacks (ai-services.ts, executiveSynthesis.ts, intentClassifier.ts, Dockerfile) are development-only, predate this work and were left alone. **Gemini was NOT shown to be perfect:** on the 11 hardest pages it read every key item that was checked (120/120 numbers, 69/69 phrases, 26/26 pairs), but those are items chosen by hand, the sample is small, and a vision model can invent a digit or stop early: so its reading is compared with the pipeline's, never trusted alone, and a reading that was cut off or looped is marked incomplete (STOPPED_MARKER; Gemini's finishReason is checked). Things to keep in mind: Gemini is an online service (page images of circulars are sent to Google; whether that is acceptable for the plant is SAIL IT's and the user's decision), it is paid per use and rate-limited, it takes about 21 s per page (so a long circular must be checked in a background step, not inside the upload request), and model names are retired from time to time (gemini-2.5-flash was on 17 Oct): keep GEMINI_VISION_MODEL current. **Step 31a: the reading check** (`readingCheck.ts`, not yet wired into upload or the database). It compares the pipeline's reading with a second reader's, on numbers (dates, amounts and references in one canonical form; Devanagari/Bengali digits read as 0-9; the pipeline's own col11: labels and [handwritten: ...] spans left out, a handwritten number can only CONFIRM, never raise a flag), with four statuses: agreed / differences / partial (second reading stopped early: what it does not cover is NOT held against it) / second_unusable; small plain numbers (under 4 digits, no separator) are counted but never raise the status; letters from a script that does not belong (Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic, CJK...) raise it. The local reader now stops a looping model after 6 identical lines (blank table rows count as identical), keeps what came before, and writes a marker; its prompt now says not to write blank form rows out (**this prompt change is not yet measured**). **Measured on your 11 real pages, pipeline against Gemini:** 5 agree outright (GPAIS English, GPAIS Hindi, football, gift-card corrigendum, merit scholarship); 6 are flagged, and the flags include real pipeline errors: the pin code 713203 read as 13203 and two telephone numbers (gate pass), the deadline 20-08-2026 read as 20-0802 and the lost circular number 2026/52 (Hindi mediclaim), the amount 25,000 read as 215,000 (Hindi gift card); the rest are unconfirmed stray numbers beside stamps or handwriting (9022, 1028, 3.1000). Against the local model: the same flags, plus the Cyrillic letters in a name, and the Hindi GPAIS page as partial. Cost to note for production: Gemini takes about 21 s per page (a 10-page circular is several minutes, so it belongs in a background step, not in the upload request) and is paid per use.

**Measured so far.** Wage notice, tesseract as before: 17 of 22 key numbers, 1 of 5 label-with-value pairs, 3 wrong readings; new pipeline: 22 of 22, 5 of 5, none. A survey of all 170 pages of your circulars: 68 pages have ruled tables, 83 of 91 tables were read, no failures, median 0.4 s per page, worst case 9 s. Before/after comparison on the table pages: see the result below once it has run.

**Known weaknesses, stated plainly.** Tesseract's own cell errors repeat across the three scales (a serial number read as "4", a narrow stacked heading read as "DA WwW"); a vote cannot fix an error the same reader makes every time, which is why the second reader exists. A plain-text page with a look-alike digit ("10 00 AM" read as "70 00 AM") is not helped by the table reader at all. The Hindi fallback could not be tested where it was written (only English language data there). Handwriting, stamps over text, and faint photocopies are not solved by any OCR.

**The layered design (decided with you, 17 Oct).** Straighten the page; read ruled tables cell by cell; read the page a second time with an independent reader (a vision model) and compare number by number; sanity-check values (a valid time, a valid date, totals that add up); flag the pages and values that disagree for a quick human check at upload; score every change against pages transcribed by hand; the answer step refuses anything unproven (done). Gemini may be used now (development, nothing classified). If SAIL ever requires the readings to stay inside its closed network, a local vision model on a GPU server would be the route (see the DECISION); that is not the current plan. `pageReader.ts` keeps the second reader behind one small interface. (The project's older Ollama fallbacks are development-only and unrelated to the page reader.)

**Next.** Run the pilot (`scripts/ocr-pilot.ts`), send the report; wire the Gemini reading check into the re-read tool (dry run first), then re-read the archive with the new pipeline.

## What your first test of answer-first showed (17 Oct), and what was done

The wage question was answered "Works Rs 20995.30 per day; Projects Rs 1924530 per day": the MONTHLY TOTALS (and one garbled number), not the daily wages 808 / 740 / 809. Two causes:

1. **The stored text of that scan is damaged.** Reproduced with the app's own path (pdf-to-img at 300 dpi, then tesseract.js, automatic layout): the narrow rightmost column (Daily Wages) is not read at all and the table's grid lines appear as "]" beside the numbers ("3350.00]", "27000] 000] 1924530"). The command-line Tesseract reads the same page correctly, and the app's table reader then writes correct labelled rows ("Area: WORKS | ... | TOTAL: 20995.30 | Wages Per Day: 808"). Tried and rejected: erasing the grid lines (the number rows then vanished); other layout modes (inconsistent).
2. **My checks of 29a were too weak for tables**: they proved a quote exists and the number is inside it, not that the number is the column the answer claims. 29b fixes this: a number from a table row must come with its column label, standing right BEFORE the number in the quote; a total can no longer be passed off as a daily wage; time words in the heading ("daily") must be said by a column. Damaged text now gives NO answer instead of a wrong one.

What is still needed so that this notice (and every scanned table) gives the right answer: a more reliable reading of table pages. Options, not built yet: (a) a vision model (Gemini, which the app already has a key for) transcribing table pages from the page image, used at upload; (b) a one-off verified correction of the stored text of a specific page. Both need your decision and (for (a)) real keys to test.

## The order we follow

1. **Test the gate** (24a) and the executive check (25a). Quick.
2. **Baseline run** of the questions that need the model (employee, health check-up, circulars, EDS). Needs a few days on the free Groq tier (about 30 to 40 model questions a day): `--max-tokens` and `--resume` handle that.
2a. **The Gemini reading check** (33a, 33b), **the confirmation gate** (35a) and **fact-based verification** (36a): built. FIRST: add the ANSWER STAGE to the acceptance harness (after the search, POST /api/ai-search/answer with the items the browser would send; record answered / refused and the reason; check expected facts), so the answer card is measured on the whole set instead of one query at a time; mind the token budget (a question costs about 5k tokens in the search stage and 1.5k to 4k in the answer stage; the free Groq tier allows about 200k a day). Next: run `10_circular_page_check.sql` in Supabase, apply 35a, `check-readings.ts --ids 49,51,82 --save`; then, in batches over the whole archive (about 25 s a page, `--max-calls` per run): re-read dry run, review, `--apply`, then `check-readings.ts --save`. Later: show "verify on the page" with the page image instead of hiding the card when the gate refuses; decide what to do with the numbers only the second reader had.
3. **Fix wrong or invented answers**, the serious ones (ledger 51 and its group): first a verified truth set from the 50 circulars, then the engine.
4. **Server-side executive check**: DONE in 25a (pending your test).
5. **Remove the EDS button**.

## Which questions belong to EDS

**EDS = the data-mining route for a policy maker**: the answer is HIDDEN until facts from several circulars are related, compared,
correlated or traced over time ("what are we missing", "where do the rules contradict", "what pattern emerges", "what changed between").
A **Smart Semantic** question asks ONE thing that ONE circular answers ("How do I return my provident fund advance before the due date?").
The detector (`src/lib/search/synthesisIntent.ts`) looks for:

1. an analytic task verb: trace / evolution, contrast, compare, synthesize, cross-reference, briefing (all four old tests start with one);
2. a relation between versions: "what changed between", "original ... corrected", "first announced", "revised after it was announced";
3. an aggregate across a period: "list the events ... in December";
4. discovery language: correlate, hidden, overlooked, inconsistencies, implications, relationship between, overlap, diverge (decisive);
   patterns, trends, recurring themes, how often, across all circulars (supporting);
5. supporting shape: two or more years, "and which / and what", many named things, length.

A question about people (who / senior / designation / ticket) is never EDS. What EDS covers TODAY: the circulars. Mining across the
employee directory, health check-up lists and holidays together would be a different engine (see "Decisions waiting").

How good is the detector? On your real questions: all 9 EDS ones detected, 0 of the 216 others taken for EDS. On 14 policy-maker
questions I wrote and did NOT tune on: **6 detected (43%)**. After I fixed the generic gaps they exposed (a relative "whose" blocked as
a people question, a bare "contradict", "first gift card announcement") it is 11 of 14, but that number is tuned and optimistic. The
three still missed have no cue words at all ("Does the safety circular undermine or reinforce the contractor insurance scheme?").
**Recall matters most, because once the button is gone a missed question cannot be sent to EDS at all**: your real typing, recorded in
the trace, is the honest measure, and it decides whether executives also need a small "analyse with EDS" link.

## Before the EDS button can go (checklist)

- [ ] The server decides who is an executive from the login session (25a).
- [x] The Enter key sends an EDS-type question from a logged-in executive to EDS, with the same processing panel and briefing as the button (27a, off by default).
- [x] ... and a synthesis with no relevant circular falls back to the normal search (27a).
- [ ] **Recall measured on real typing** (trace) and the fallback for missed questions decided (see below).
- [ ] The "Executive Deep Synthesis" title moves inside the processing panel and the briefing header (today the amber button bar carries it).
- [ ] EDS answers never contain a number that is in no circular (ledger 51), checked against a verified truth set.
- [ ] The acceptance set passes at a rate you agree, through the real route.
- [ ] Every EDS failure is labelled route / engine / display.

## Decisions waiting for you

- **A bare "how many holidays"** (no year) silently uses the current year (F-20). Keep, or ask which year?
- **Compound questions** ("how many RH can category B take in 2025 and which fall in November") answer the first half and say "Not answered" for the second. Keep, or answer both?
- **Decided by me (13 Oct), tell me if you disagree**: (1) EDS is for logged-in executives only; (2) the screen, not the server, decides to start it, and the server still verifies the executive; (3) when the button is removed, its amber title bar goes with it, so "Executive Deep Synthesis" becomes the title of the processing panel and of the briefing header (the existing small label is kept); (4) a question the detector misses cannot reach EDS once the button is gone: if the real typing shows misses, executives get a small "analyse with Executive Deep Synthesis" link under circular results.
- *(answered)* EDS will cover every domain (see "Direction" above): you decide the order of the domains as data arrives; I decide nothing about who may see payroll or medical except that the default is deny.

## How to test things

- `node scripts/omnibar-acceptance.mjs --list` : what is in the set.
- `--no-model` : the free part (holidays, Bad Queries). `--max-tokens N`, `--resume FILE` : spread the model part over days.
- Gate: `FRONT_PATH_SKIP_MODEL=true` in `.env.local`, then see `eval/README.md`.
- Synthesis and the security probes: `--cookie "next-auth.session-token=..."` (see `eval/README.md`).
- Every search prints a `[front-path] ...` line in the dev terminal: route, stage times, model calls, tokens.

The priority is a real weight, not only list order: in the merge each paper takes `weight` places per round, so a full screen of 15 is 3+3+3+2+2+1+1 when every paper has enough fresh stories (tested), and papers of equal weight get equal shares. A STORY BELONGS TO THE PAPER OF HIGHEST PRIORITY THAT CARRIED IT (so a story first reported by a lower paper and carried later by The Telegraph counts as The Telegraph's, and is named 'first reported by ...'); among equals it goes to the one that reported first, so that list order favours none (tested). | 155 | AICPI-IW graph and the quarterly dearness allowance of SAIL employees (card "aicpi da" / "sail da") | DATA: the Labour Bureau (labourbureau.gov.in), two public HTML pages, no key: the CPI-IW page (its script holds the last twelve months) and the home page box (latest two months), merged into 13 monthly figures (Sep 2025 to Sep 2026 on 13 Nov 2026), the span of the other one-year charts; the pages must agree on a shared month; kept six hours; the October index is due 30 Nov 2026. THE DA IS THE CPSE IDA PATTERN OF THE 2017 PAY SCALES, REVISED EVERY QUARTER, from DPE orders: OM No. W-02/0028/2017-DPE(WC)-GL-XIII/17 of 3 Aug 2017 para 7 (100% neutralisation, link point 277.33 = average of AICPI 2001=100 for Sep to Nov 2016, DA zero on 1.1.2017, adjusted once in three months; text read from copies on govtempdiary.com and igecorner.com) and OM F.No. W-02/0039/2017-DPE(WC)-GL-V/20 of 3 Apr 2020 (1 April 2020: average of Dec to Feb 329.33, increase 52, DA 18.7% [52/277.33 x 100]). FORMULA (src/lib/live/aicpi.ts): IDA % = ((average of 3 months x 2.88) - 277.33) / 277.33 x 100, one decimal, rounded to the NEAREST tenth; quarters: 1 Jan = Sep to Nov, 1 Apr = Dec to Feb, 1 Jul = Mar to May, 1 Oct = Jun to Aug. The factor 2.88 (base 2016 to base 2001) is not printed in the orders; it was VERIFIED BY REPRODUCING THE ORDERS: the DPE OM No. W-02/0037/2025-DPE(WC)/FTS-14505 of 27 Oct 2026 (read as a page image from a copy on dtf.in) gives 59.1% for the 2017 scale from 1.10.2026 (computed 59.06), and a Coal India circular of 6 Mar 2026 gives 53.4% from 1.1.2026 (computed 53.42); third-party rates 54.1 (Apr 2026) and 55.7 (Jul 2026) also agree. Other pay scales in the 27 Oct order (1997: 489.5%, 2007: 249.2%) are not worked out. WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED: that SAIL pays exactly this. Evidence: SAIL executives are under the DPE guidelines; the NJCS MoU of 22 Oct 2021 (implemented from 18.11.2021 per SAIL's Rajya Sabha reply) is reported (search summary of a SAIL document, and a Business League article of 4 Jan 2022: executives and non-executives 27.2% to 29.4%) to give the non-executives the same DA with link point 277.33 base 2001=100, DA nil on 1.1.2017. SAIL's own pages could NOT be read: sail.co.in fails its TLS certificate check (not bypassed), the BSE filings answer 403 (not bypassed). No SAIL-specific extra factor was found and none is included; the card says SAIL's own circular is the authority. OWNER'S GUESS that SAIL has its own formula with other factors: nothing found supports it; if SAIL has a circular with different rules, paste it and the card is changed and tested against it. CARD: latest index, 13-month graph, the quarter in force (59.1% from 1 Oct 2026, with its DPE order), the next (expected 61.5% from 1 Jan 2027 if Oct and Nov stand at 155.5), one index point = 1.04% of DA, a table of the quarters with the work of each and the order ticked where read, the formula and the work for the current quarter, a basic-pay calculator (rate x basic pay to the nearest rupee, pay slip is the authority). THE ANNOUNCED RATES (53.4 from 1 Jan 2026, 59.1 from 1 Oct 2026) ARE A SMALL HAND TABLE (IDA_ANNOUNCED, compared 13 Nov 2026): add a row at each DPE order (each quarter); other quarters are computed. The central government DA built first (58, 60, 63% by the Finance Ministry orders, formula (avg x 2.88 - 261.42)/261.42, verified the same way) was REMOVED from the card as not SAIL's; the verification stays in this note. Tests: aicpi.selftest 26, AicpiCard.selftest 19. | 162 | Local (MEMU) train FARE HINT from the railway's published fare table | The reservation system has no fare for a train with unreserved seats only (its schedule says "category":"Local","isPrsBookable":false), so each local train that goes on from Durgapur has a "₹ Fare hint": the OFFICIAL table "Basic fare of ORDINARY SERVICES w.e.f. 26.12.2025" (Railway Board Commercial Circular No. 24 of 2025, 25 Dec 2025; annex pages 5-6 of the scanned PDF at staffnews.in, read by eye on 14 Nov 2026), NON-SUBURBAN SECOND CLASS, column "Minimum Chargeable fare" = the ticket price (the basic fare rounded up to a multiple of Rs 5, minimum Rs 10: the test checks this on all 80 rows, so a misreading shows), typed in src/lib/live/ordinaryFare.ts (1-500 km). DISTANCE: Durgapur to the end station = difference of two cumulative distances in RailRadar GET /v1/trains/{n}?haltsOnly=true (1 request per train). RailRadar's distances differ from the railway's own by 0.4-6.5 km (checked on 4 trains against the fare service's integer distance), so the hint is worked out for the distance +/- max(2 km, 1%) and SHOWN AS A RANGE when a fare step lies inside it. SHOWN as "Fare hint ... about Rs X", disclaimer "An estimate, not a lookup", source = the circular + RailRadar (distance), the day it was applied (never called old/frozen: a fixed table does not age as a fare does). A MEMU with an express number (13xxx) is warned that the railway may charge the higher mail/express rate (NOT worked out). Examples: Durgapur-Asansol 42.3 km = Rs 10; Durgapur-Sealdah (13180) 177.6 km = Rs 40. NOT KNOWN: whether the railway treats Durgapur's MEMUs as non-suburban (the circular only names AC MEMU/DEMU as an exception; Durgapur is outside Kolkata's suburban system, so the non-suburban column is used), and any revision after 26 Dec 2025 (ORDINARY_TABLE_EFFECTIVE). Saved in train_fare like the other fares, so it keeps working after the paid month. Files: fare.ts (askLocalFare, parseLocalDistance), fareService.ts (local flag), railway-fare route, Fares.tsx (HintDisclaimer), RailwayCard.tsx, scripts/harvest-fares.ts (also collects the locals, 1 request each). Tests: ordinaryFare 13, fare 22, fareService 13, RailwayCard 50. NOT BUILT: an unreserved fare for the EXPRESS trains (second class mail/express table and superfast charge are in the same circular, later pages). |
